## Document 003: 10 questions for China in 2027

Source PDF: all report pdf/10 questions for China in 2027.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-11
Pages: 24
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

2026 has been a year of slowing growth and increasing
divergence within China's economy. We saw the gap between
external and domestic demand, as well as between the new
economy and the old economy, widen signiﬁcantly. Here, we
look ahead to the key questions facing China in 2027
Lynn Song
Beijing, China
1) Will China’s growth target be lowered
again?
Despite a growing emphasis on the quality of growth rather than
just the pace, China’s annual economic target-setting exercise in

March remains one of the year's most important events. The GDP
growth target and ﬁscal quotas tend to determine the level of
policy support needed in the year ahead.
China very rarely misses its growth target. Since 1991, it has fallen
signiﬁcantly short of target only once – after the widespread
pandemic lockdowns in 2022.

### Page 2

In 2026, lowering the growth target from “around 5%” to 4.5-5.0%
set the stage for slower activity and less urgency for stimulus than
in previous years. We expect the target to be achieved, but growth
will likely be toward the low end of the range.
The key question for 2027 is whether we’ll see the growth target
soften further. China’s economy has seen growing imbalances over
the past year. Next year’s growth will rely heavily on external
demand and new-economy drivers. Uncertainty about whether

external demand and the AI-driven tech capex boom can continue
could aﬀect overall growth.
We expect a slight softening of the growth target to “around 4.5%”.
This would allow for decent policy ﬂexibility while keeping the long-
term challenges in mind. Our calculations show China will need to
grow at an average of around 4% per year to achieve the 2030
(RMB170tr) and 2035 (RMB207tr) targets set by Premier Li Qiang
and President Xi Jinping, respectively. A target of “around 4.5%”

gives China some buﬀer room if growth slows more signiﬁcantly
going forward.
Accordingly, we expect growth to come in at 4.5% year-on-year in
2027, broadly in line with our 4.6% forecast for 2026. This will
maintain China’s role as a key driver of global growth. China is
expected to contribute somewhere between a ﬁfth and a quarter of
global growth over the next ﬁve years.
China's growth target may be adjusted to
"around 4.5%" in 2027

### Page 3

2) Will external demand and tech drive
growth again?
We observed two major K-shaped trends in 2026: the divergence
between strong external demand and weak domestic demand, and
the split between the tech-led new economy and the old economy.
We expect export growth to moderate but remain relatively strong
next year, with high-single-digit increases in exports. While things
are likely to normalise a bit after an extraordinarily strong year in
2026, external demand will still remain one of China's key growth

engines.
First, strong export performance in 2026 will create a challenging
base eﬀect to follow in 2027. The run-up in energy and technology
prices that underpinned growth is also unlikely to be repeated,
reducing the scope for a similar boost next year.
Second, uncertainty could return in 2027 after what has been a
relatively calm year in terms of trade developments following a
roller-coaster 2025.
The China-US trade truce at the time of writing is set to expire in

January 2027. While our base case is a further extension of this
agreement, risks of re-escalation can’t be ruled out, particularly
as China’s trade surplus widened 12% YoY to $233.8bn through
the ﬁrst three quarters of 2026. The ‘blue wave’ election
outcome in the US could lead to gridlock and prompt US
President Donald Trump to focus on areas where he can
exercise executive power, such as trade policy.

### Page 4

Possible trade tensions with the EU are also worth considering.
ASEAN has overtaken the US as China’s largest export
destination. The EU remains second, representing 14-15% of
China’s total exports. China’s trade surplus with the EU surged
26.1% YoY to $277.4bn in the ﬁrst three quarters of 2026. After
trade talks in October, China and the EU agreed to a 16-point
plan to improve trade ties. The plan includes a cap on exports
of hybrid vehicles into the EU at 2025 levels. Almost

immediately, calls emerged to extend the quotas to other
industries. Almost immediately after, there were additional
calls to extend these quotas to other industries. While we
believe there’s plenty of room for win-win cooperation, there’s
also risk of trade friction increasing in the year ahead.
On the tech side, 2026 marked an acceleration of the capex cycle
and AI boom. Hi-tech investment was one of the few areas that saw
positive investment growth in 2026, and tech-related exports and

industrial production also outperformed on the year.
However, we also saw several notable market pullbacks along with
rising fears of an AI bubble bursting. One of the biggest questions
for not just China but the global economy at large is whether
impressive tech growth continues into 2027.
At this juncture, the AI-driven investment boom looks likely to
continue into 2027, with many companies still reporting order
books full through 2027. We think the current engines of growth will

moderate but continue to drive growth into 2027. To achieve stable
growth, other parts of the economy might need to pick up the
slack. Our next question deals with whether domestic demand can
step up to the plate.
Technology products and export
diversiﬁcation continue to support external
demand

### Page 5

3) Can domestic demand carry more of the
load?
Anyone following Chinese policymakers' messaging will have
noticed a recurring theme in recent years: the need to increase the
role of domestic demand in driving growth.
China’s 15th Five-Year Plan places building a strong domestic
market among its top priorities, alongside industrial
modernisation, tech self-reliance, and the digital economy. Yet
while the latter three have been obvious recipients of policy backing

and resources, the push to strengthen domestic demand has often
appeared less tangible. These categories are clearly policy
favourites and receive more resources.
However, 2026 was one of the weakest years for domestic demand
in recent history. Over the ﬁrst three quarters, China’s retail sales
grew just 1.1% YoY ytd.
In our view, several factors played a major role in weak
consumption growth in 2026:
Trade-in programme turned from tailwind into headwind.

China’s ﬂagship policy to boost consumption was the trade-in
programme. It front-loaded consumption by oﬀering consumer
subsidies for targeted products across categories including
autos, household appliances, furniture, and communication
devices. The programme's scale fell slightly in 2026, which
along with previous front-loading clearly dragged retail sales
growth, with household appliance, furniture, and auto sales
down year on year.
Consumer conﬁdence ﬂatlined after a modest recovery in

2025. Conﬁdence peaked in February 2026, but pulled back

### Page 6

afterwards. Low wage growth, combined with a negative
wealth eﬀect from falling equity and property prices, was the
main culprit for soft conﬁdence. Weak conﬁdence continued to
reduce discretionary consumption categories for most of the
year.
The peak and subsequent drop of gold prices dragged gold &
jewellery sales. Gold prices peaked in January. The subsequent
bust signiﬁcantly reduced retail interest in buying gold.
Heading into 2027, we expect a modest recovery in consumption,

with potential upside for a larger recovery.
Drag from trade-in programme should be smaller. The
pullback we saw from the household appliances, furniture, and
auto categories should be smaller in 2027. It’s too early to
expect signiﬁcant replacement demand for appliances and
furniture, but auto sales could begin to see some replacement
demand from the ﬁrst wave of EV sales. This could reduce the
category's drag. However, given the strength of communication

appliance sales in 2026, this category is ripe for a 2027
pullback.
Policy support for domestic demand could pick up, especially
if the external demand picture worsens. While it seems
reasonable to expect resources to remain concentrated toward
tech thanks to the AI race, supporting domestic demand could
become more important if we see signs of the external demand
engine slowing in 2027. Longer term, increasing domestic
demand is necessary to achieve more sustainable growth.

The big wildcard is how consumer conﬁdence fares next year. The
main factors will likely be wage growth and asset price
performance. We’ve seen some encouraging signs that property
prices are beginning to bottom out in tier 1 and a few tier 2 cities.
This could start to translate into a conﬁdence recovery. While wage
growth has remained slow amid a weak job market, a recovery of
corporate proﬁts could potentially translate into a recovery if the
trend holds.

### Page 7

Weak consumer conﬁdence remains the
main constraint on domestic demand
4) Has China successfully escaped
deﬂation?
China’s struggle with deﬂation in the past few years triggered
signiﬁcant debate about whether China was headed down the
same path as Japan’s Lost Decades. Wealth destruction from the
property market bust, aggressive price competition, and poor
domestic demand contributed to three straight years of near-zero
inﬂation. It also threatened to put China into a negative feedback

loop of falling prices and proﬁtability, lower wage growth, and
weaker consumption and investment.
However, in 2026 we ﬁnally saw a more convincing uptick in
inﬂation, ending the year above 0% for the ﬁrst time since 2022.
We think the worst of the deﬂationary pressure may be behind us.
Looking ahead to 2027, several factors are likely to continue
shaping the inﬂation outlook.
Food and housing have been the two biggest drags on inﬂation
over the past year, together accounting for more than half of

China's CPI basket. The drag from food should gradually fade as
pork prices eventually stabilise. Pork has started to see sequential
increases starting in the second half of 2026. Abundant domestic
supplies of fresh vegetables and fruits should continue thanks to
greenhouse expansion, but likely won't repeat the same
deﬂationary impact next year.

### Page 8

Rent may continue to drag on inﬂation until we see a broader
bottom in the property market. Rent has been at -0.6% YoY since
April 2026.
This year’s primary inﬂation driver was energy prices, as fallout
from the Iran war hit the global economy. Energy costs are a
wildcard in 2027. Our house forecasts Brent averaging $81/bbl in
2027, which should keep inﬂation elevated after higher prices in
2026.
Tech prices, by contrast, surged in 2026 as the AI boom drove

heavy demand and price hikes across many tech supply chains.
This was most evident in the communication appliances
component of China's CPI basket, where prices rose 6.3% YoY in the
ﬁrst three quarters.
Furthermore, China’s PPI inﬂation surged in 2026. Producer prices
rose amid higher raw material and other input prices. Companies
have been reluctant to pass higher costs on to consumers amid
subdued conﬁdence. But if input costs remain elevated into next

year, maintaining that restraint may become increasingly diﬃcult.
Overall, we expect China’s CPI inﬂation to move a little higher next
year, to 1.1% YoY. This level is a sweet spot for China, staying a
comfortable distance from deﬂation while avoiding the high
inﬂation pressure many other economies are currently facing. At
this level, we don’t expect inﬂation to constrain monetary easing if
needed.
Inﬂation has turned higher, but the recovery
remains uneven

### Page 9

5) Will the drag from the property market
ﬁnally fade?
Barring an astounding end-of-year turnaround, property prices will
have fallen for a ﬁfth consecutive year by the time 2026 wraps up.
China's property downturn has evolved considerably over the past
few years. Fears of a catastrophic systemic crisis have largely
faded, and developer defaults have become less frequent. Yet home
prices still haven’t found a clear ﬂoor, and the pessimism unleashed

by the housing slump remains deeply embedded in household
sentiment. The property market remains a major overhang on the
economy.
Building sales and real estate ﬁxed-asset investment continued to
see steep declines, down -13.9% YoY and -20.6%, respectively.
The indirect impact of the property bust is harder to measure, but
it’s widespread and arguably even more signiﬁcant.
Consumer conﬁdence has been weak since the property
downturn began, and the recovery has been slow and uneven

since.
Adjacent industries have been impacted. Retail sales of
furniture (-4.7%), household appliance sales (-4.9%), and
building & decoration materials (-9.7%) are in contraction this
year. Industrial production data shows signiﬁcant weakness in
property-related categories such as cement (-8.6%), ﬂat glass
(-7.0%), and steel (-2.0%) through the third quarter.
Land sales traditionally have been a major source of local
government revenue. This dried up as demand plummeted

amid the property bust. Local-government revenue from
transfers of state-owned land-use rights fell from CNY8.7tn in
2021 to CNY4.15tn in 2025, and looks to have fallen further in
2026. The consequence of this is that local governments are
also tightening their belts in terms of spending.
Falling home and rental prices are contributing to deﬂationary
pressure.
Is the drag from the property sector going to fade in 2027?

### Page 10

In our view, two things need to happen before we see a property
sector turnaround. First, we need to see signs that price
stabilisation is becoming more entrenched. This year has delivered
the most encouraging evidence since the property downturn
began, with prices in tier-one cities broadly stabilising or rising for
much of the year. If that trend persists and spreads to more tier-two
and tier-three cities, it could mark the bottom of the current cycle.

For now, the 70-city average property price index has yet to
establish a bottom.
Second, property inventories remain elevated, reaching a new
record high in early 2026 before dropping later in the year. Until
these return to more normal levels, it's diﬃcult to expect signiﬁcant
new property investment.
Policy support for the property market has expanded modestly in
2026, including a mortgage subsidy for ﬁrst-time buyers, further
loosening of purchase restrictions across the country, and

continuing with urban renewal projects.
We also saw some structural reforms. These include tightening
restrictions on the property pre-sale model, such as requiring
developers to reach a certain stage of completion before pre-sales
and requiring funds to enter a supervised account. These steps
make the operating environment more challenging for developers
but should boost buyer conﬁdence.
With further support for the property market, we think we could see

a smaller drag from the sector in 2027. If price stabilisation
continues, it would be a big step, removing a major weight on
household and investment conﬁdence. That said, with inventories
still high and developers limiting new projects, real estate
investment will likely remain a headwind next year.
High inventories and falling prices continue
to weigh on the property market

### Page 11

6) What can we expect from policymakers
in 2027?
With the 2026 growth target lowered and attention consumed by
the AI race, it is perhaps unsurprising that policy support for the
broader economy has been underwhelming this year. We expect a
generally supportive policy environment for the year ahead, with
the possibility for more aggressive policy rollout to bolster the
domestic economy if we see trade tensions pick back up next year.
On ﬁscal policy, we expect the key ﬁscal targets to remain fairly

stable in 2027.
The ﬁscal deﬁcit target is likely to remain around 4% of GDP for
a third straight year. Adjustments to this target would be
primarily for signalling purposes rather than to actively
constrain policy.
Our base case for bond issuance targets is that they remain
little changed, around RMB 4.4tn for special local government
bonds and RMB 1.3tn for ultra-long-term bonds. Given the
limited appetite for using approved quotas in 2026 amid the

drop in investment, it's possible that targets could be lowered.
Our base case is for targets to stay high to increase ﬂexibility.
On monetary policy, we expect the People’s Bank of China to buck
global trends and maintain an easing bias. Inﬂation remains well
below target, the labour market remains fairly soft, growth has
been decelerating, and the CNY has been strengthening. Conditions
are mostly suitable for further easing, with the key exception being

bank net interest margins are already at historical lows, and
policymakers have thus far preferred targeted easing instead.
Below are our key calls for the PBOC next year:

### Page 12

We look for a single 10bp rate cut to the benchmark rate. The
PBOC has resisted broad-based rate cuts, mindful of low
banking-sector margins and the fact that many major central
banks have remained in hiking mode.
Targeted measures to support selected industries look likely
to continue. Re-lending programmes, interest rate subsidies,
and risk-sharing facilities may remain the main tools for
allocating money to areas of need.
PBOC benchmark rate shift will improve monetary policy

transmission. The PBOC formalised a shift away from the 7-
day reverse repo rate to the overnight reverse repo rate starting
2 November 2026. We expect this shift to improve monetary
policy transmission, enable more precise day-to-day liquidity
management, and move China’s framework closer to global
peers.
At the sector level, we expect policy priorities in 2027 to remain
closely aligned with those laid out in the Five-Year Plan, with
industrial modernisation and technological self-reliance continuing

to attract the lion's share of attention and resources.
Given recent communications, we may also see more concerted
eﬀorts to build out domestic demand. An October National
Development and Reform Commission (NDRC) news conference
oﬀered some food for thought, promising to “strengthen inclusive
policies that reach consumers directly” alongside existing strategies
to expand and upgrade goods such as new energy vehicles and
smart products. One limitation of recent consumption-support

measures is that, while trade-in programmes have successfully
brought forward spending, they have done little to lift household
conﬁdence. The next step may be to recalibrate policy to make
consumers feel genuinely better oﬀ.
Support for the property market should also continue to spread the
initial recovery seen in the core cities nationwide.
Policy will remain supportive, but broad
stimulus is unlikely

### Page 13

7) Will CNY two-way moves return after
2026's gains?
We set an initial forecast band for the USDCNY in 2027 of 6.40-6.80,
and our 2027 year-end forecast set at 6.50.
2026 marked another strong year for the CNY, as the appreciation
momentum started in 2025 and continued into 2026 despite what
has largely been a strong dollar backdrop. Generally, appreciation
has been stronger than what most market participants had
expected at the start of the year.

As the numbers above imply, our base case has appreciation
continuing. Our key reasons are listed below:
We continue to hear a very strong consensus among market
participants for a moderate pace of appreciation to continue.
While this raises risks of a sharper reversal, it also remains
diﬃcult to bet against.
Exporters continue to sell into upticks of the USDCNY, creating a
convincing environment for CNY appreciation to continue.
Considering the foreign currency left abroad over the past few

years, and incoming foreign currency receivables with China’s
trade surplus set to hit a new record high in 2026 and remain
strong in 2027, the stage is set for further CNY appreciation.
PBOC has signalled it has neither the need nor intention to use
currency depreciation to gain advantages in trade. This
suggests that there won't be a push to depreciate the currency
even if the current trade boom cools oﬀ.
CNY strength has thus far been without the support of equity

market inﬂows. Chinese markets have been an underperformer.
Your privacy is important

### Page 14

But if there's some mean reversion or a bull market reversal,
this could further support CNY strength.
However, we don't think 2027 will see a repeat of this relatively
simple straight line appreciation from April 2025 to the time of
writing. Two-way ﬂuctuation looks likely to return, and the recent
CNY appreciation bias in markets raises the risk for a stronger
reversal, even if the PBOC acts to limit excessive volatility.
US-China yield spreads remain near-record highs. This

correlation weakened in 2026 as investors became worried
about missing out on further appreciation, and as the spread
narrowed to start the year. However, a hawkish shift as the Iran
war broke out has widened the yield spread to new highs. This
should eventually tempt investors.
PBOC could step in if the appreciation trend goes too far. In an
October policy statement, the PBOC warned against one-sided
and procyclical behaviour in the FX market, reﬂecting concern

that market participants can reinforce an exchange-rate move
through a herding eﬀect. In early November, the PBOC raised
the foreign-exchange deposit reserve requirement from 4% to
6%. It was widely seen as a warning against excessive yuan
appreciation and one-sided speculative positioning,
discouraging this behaviour by draining foreign-currency
liquidity from the banking system.
Outward direct investment looks to be closing a year with an
uncharacteristic year-on-year decline after seeing double-digit

growth for 5 of the past 6 years. A recovery of ODI demand
could add selling pressure to the CNY.
CNY appreciation should continue, but with
greater two-way volatility

### Page 15

8) Are CGBs the biggest asymmetric risk to
Chinese markets?
China’s government bond yields bucked global trends in 2026,
falling despite a surge in yields elsewhere in the world amid rising
inﬂation and debt.
The falling yields have been a double-edged sword. On one hand,
low yields have allowed the government to issue debt cheaply, an
important beneﬁt as China brings shadow-banking debt onto
oﬃcial balance sheets, and as debt levels remain high. However, the

low yields have also become a symbol of weak investor conﬁdence
and a factor cited by those arguing that China is seeing entrenched
pessimism and on a path of “Japanisation.”
China’s government bond yields have generally trended downward
since 2020, with pockets of volatility throughout. Several key
factors have contributed to this long bull market:
Investor risk appetite remains abnormally soft. Although we
would argue that China exited “peak pessimism” in 2024,

many market participants still maintain very conservative
asset allocations. Equity and property markets continue to
underperform, and demand for risk-free assets remains huge.
Limited credit demand has led to rising bank holdings of CGBs.
Bank holdings of CGBs have nearly quadrupled over the past
decade and stood at RMB 78.6tn at the end of 3Q26.
Many remain trapped in a deﬂationary mindset. China has
taken steps to move out of deﬂation in 2026, thanks to higher

energy prices that boosted core inﬂation. Yet the deﬂationary
mindset persists for many, contributing to a willingness to
accept such low yields.

### Page 16

Tighter restrictions on outbound investment may be
preventing investors from investing in higher-yielding bonds
abroad. China’s yields are now signiﬁcantly lower than most
major economies, but domestic investors still face limitations
on how much they can move abroad to capitalise on this
spread. Capital controls remain a major factor in how yields
can stay so low.
We think there’s a solid case for bond yields to turn around in 2027.
A slight normalisation of risk appetite would be suﬃcient to

cause a major shift. A return to a more balanced asset
allocation would be a catalyst for risk asset rallies and a bond
market pullback. Banks are investing in bonds instead of
lending thanks to unusually weak investment appetite. A
reversion to the mean would be the biggest catalyst for a bond
selloﬀ.
China’s long-term growth and inﬂation outlook don’t match
the current yields. Current yields imply a deﬂationary,
stagnant environment, while most economists expect

moderating but still solid growth and low but positive inﬂation.
Time will tell which side is right, but bond yields suggest
markets are pricing in a signiﬁcantly more downbeat outlook
than what the data is showing.
Bond issuance is expected to remain high, and debt levels
remain elevated. As we discussed earlier in this report, China’s
ﬁscal policy stance is likely to remain accommodative. Another
year of high bond issuance should add some supply pressure

on the CGB market.
Higher global yields make Chinese yields even less attractive
by comparison. China’s bond yields are now among the lowest
of major economies. Capital controls will limit how far investors
can chase higher yields, but an asset allocation shift from
domestic to global bonds could still play a role.
Policymakers may start to worry about the implications of
low long-end yields, and push back if the bull market
continues. Recall that back in 2024, the PBOC had repeatedly

intervened to keep the 10-year CGB yield above 2%. While
these eﬀorts were discontinued, market rumours at the end of

October suggested that policymakers had asked Chinese
ﬁnancial institutions not to add duration in the bond market.
We look for 10-year CGB yields to rise to 1.9% by the end of 2027.
The key risks to this outlook are further worsening sentiment, a
return of deﬂation, more aggressive-than-expected rate cuts, or
tighter capital controls that keep domestic money locked up in the
CGB market.
Heavy bond issuance has been absorbed by
rising bank demand

9) How is the AI boom impacting China?
AI has become one of China’s more important growth engines,
supporting exports, manufacturing, investment and productivity. At
the same time, the heavy focus on the AI race means it is also a
major driver of the K-shaped divergence in China. It's likely to
continue to widen the gap between industries and companies.
China’s strong trade growth on both the export and import sides
were in large part tied to AI-related demand. Over 6,000 Chinese AI

ﬁrms across AI infrastructure, framework, and applications
contributed to heavy demand. We saw categories such as
automatic data processing machines, semiconductors, and general
computer & electronics with high double-digit and even triple-digit
export and import growth.
A similar pattern was observed in China’s manufacturing industry.
Technology-intensive manufacturing signiﬁcantly outperformed
the broader industrial in 2026, led by electronic equipment and

semiconductors. Chinese companies across a broad range of

### Page 18

industries are integrating AI into quality control, from precision
electronics manufacturing to chilli oil. AI is already playing a role in
China’s industrial upgrading process.
Hi-tech investment has been the lone bright spot in an otherwise
down year for investment, with hi-tech FAI up 4.9% YoY but
headline FAI down -7.2% YoY through 3Q26. Despite an equity
market selloﬀ as investors start to question valuations, the AI capex
cycle itself looks to have further room to run. Spending by major

Chinese tech companies has doubled from their 2022 levels, with
continued investment in computing capacity, data centres and AI
infrastructure. China’s grid investment data has been volatile but
has generally shown strong growth for the last few years as well.
The impact of AI on the labour market is a hot topic globally. In
China, aggregate employment data have yet to show a broad AI-
driven shock, but ﬁrm-level evidence already points to higher

output per worker and lower labour requirements in selected tasks.
The initial impact is therefore more likely to appear through slower
hiring and job redesign than immediate mass layoﬀs.
We think two factors could make this adjustment particularly
important for China in 2027:
AI adoption could move faster in China. China’s AI strategy
places greater emphasis on industrial deployment and
applications, while public trust and workplace usage are
relatively high compared to global peers. An ageing population

also gives ﬁrms a stronger incentive to automate in response
to further labour shortages. Faster AI adoption has shown
signs of supporting labour productivity growth. It has
accelerated for two straight years to reach 6.3% in 2025 after
dropping as low as 4.4% in 2023. But it could also accelerate
the displacement of substitutable roles and increase downward
occupational mobility.
Entry-level employment will face the greatest pressure. China
is expected to have 12.7 million university graduates in 2026,

480,000 more than in 2025, adding to an already competitive
youth labour market. At the same time, AI is increasingly able
Your privacy is important
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.
You can review and change your preferences at any time in
our Cookie policy.
Essential
Performance Cookies
Targeting Cookies

### Page 19

to perform the information intensive and administrative work
which traditionally assigned to junior employees. Firms may
therefore reduce entry-level hiring without cutting their existing
workforce, narrowing the career ladder through which young
workers acquire skills and experience.
The government has rolled out policies to address the potential
impact of AI on employment, focusing on displacement risk
assessment, digital skills training, worker transitions and the

creation of new occupations. We expect these measures to
moderate, but not prevent, pressure on junior and routine white-
collar employment. The key issue for next year is whether new
service-sector and AI-complementary jobs can be created quickly
enough to absorb aﬀected workers.
Overall, we expect AI to support productivity growth and industrial
upgrading in 2027, but the gains will remain uneven. If commercial
returns disappoint the market’s high expectations, a reassessment

of the global AI cycle could weaken related demand. This could
leave China with stronger productive capacity but divergence across
industries and labour markets.
The other scenario worth considering is if we see a widespread AI
bust. Increasingly, market participants may increasingly shift their
focus to commercial returns of AI, where there is arguably still a lot
of froth in the markets. Given China’s heavy investment in the AI
race, and its prominent role in the outperforming areas of the

economy, a reversal of this trend would naturally have signiﬁcant
implications. We’ve already seen a signiﬁcant selloﬀ in equities,
which does not help the already soft conﬁdence. A moderate
pullback may not necessarily be bad news, as it could allow for
more balanced resource allocation elsewhere, but a crash would
pose larger risks to the growth outlook.

after 2023

10) How is China faring in its Great
Transition?
Our key long-term call back when ﬁrst joining ING’s research
department in 2024 was that China was not in a Great Decline, but

Your privacy is important

Essential

Targeting Cookies

### Page 21

a Great Transition. At that time, discourse on China was arguably at
its most pessimistic, with many investors deeming China
uninvestable and amid worries over systemic risks. China’s
reopening after the pandemic fell short of expectations, and many
worried its outlook was doomed.
Almost three years later, China is in a signiﬁcantly diﬀerent place.
While a cloud of pessimism still hangs over the economy at large,
the narrative has become a lot more balanced. Within China’s

widening K-shaped trajectories, pockets of extremely strong growth
remain.
We outlined four main directions for China’s Great Transition:
Developing domestic demand and consumption as a growth
driver
Moving up the value-added ladder
Strengthening the green transition
Advancing tech and digitalisation
We’ve since seen progress across all four fronts, though the speed
has been uneven.
Developing domestic demand is arguably the most disappointing
of the four key transitions so far. Chinese households’ willingness

to save has hit a new high, and weak conﬁdence continues to
weigh on this eﬀort. Retail sales slowed to a post-pandemic low in
2026. Critics of China often cite weak domestic demand as a main
factor behind growing trade imbalances.
Policymakers have said all the right things. But for now, eﬀorts to
buoy domestic demand seem to have taken a backseat to
industrial modernisation and the tech race. We do, however, see
progress in the right direction. For example, trade-in programmes

helped front-load consumption, and policymakers have vowed to
“strengthen inclusive policies that reach consumers directly.”
China has already made great progress in moving up the value-
added ladder. The evolution of China’s industrial production and
exports shows its manufacturing backbone moving from the low
end toward the high end.

### Page 22

Exports show that the share of machinery and transport equipment
in total exports rose from 48.6% in 2023 to 56.0% in 3Q26.
Traditionally low-end miscellaneous manufacturing, including
clothing and footwear, has dropped from 22.8% in 2023 to 18.6%
in 3Q26.
This transition has major global implications, prompting discussion
of a so-called “China Shock 2.0” as the economy produces
increasingly high-end products. China's auto industry has rapidly
risen to global prominence, capitalising on the accelerating

worldwide adoption of electric vehicles.
China’s green transition has continued its great progress. China
has undergone a remarkable green transition over the past decade-
plus. Various indicators point to continued success in this green
transition in recent years. China’s PM 2.5 levels across 168 cities hit
a record low of 15 in July 2026. Renewable and nuclear power
generation as a percentage of total energy production rose from
21.5% at the end of 2023 to 26.2% at the end of 2025.

The green transition remains an important medium- and long-term
goal. As such, it will likely continue to outperform in terms of
growth. In 2025, President Xi pledged to lower emissions by 7-10%
by 2035 while “striving to do better”, building on 2020 comments
aiming to achieve peak carbon by 2030 and reach carbon
neutralisation by 2060.
Heavy investment continues in green industries. China is a leading
player in key sectors such as EVs, solar, and wind power generation.

This shift is clearly aﬀecting the global economy through trade and
investment.
China’s eﬀorts to advance technological development and
digitalisation have exceeded our expectations. Chinese ﬁrms have
proven to be competitive in the AI race. China has taken major
steps to improve self-reliance, including strengthening the domestic
semiconductor industry and developing the robotics industry.
Digitalisation of the economy continues to build on existing

strength. According to China’s National Data Administration, the

core industries of China’s digital economy accounted for over

There’s also a ﬁrm commitment to continue heavy investment in
research and development. China’s 15  Five-Year Plan committed
to setting an R&D expenditure growth target of at least 7% per year
through 2026-2030. R&D spending reached RMB 3.92tn in 2025,

equivalent to 2.8% of GDP.
In conclusion, China has made good but uneven progress in its key
economic transitions over the past few years. Strong progress in the
tech and green transitions, along with China’s aggressive move up
the value-added ladder, laid a foundation for future growth.
However, domestic demand must be strengthened to drive more
sustainable long-term growth. This is particularly true as we see
increasing pushback from trading partners about China’s growing

trade surplus. In China’s “dual circulation” strategy, the internal
circulation side must be strengthened in the coming months and
years.

article.

### Page 2

 AI市場の成長期待を背景に、競争優位の確保に向けた巨額投資と、それに伴うAI技術の高
度化が進展
 投資資金の確保に向けて、社債発行など外部資金調達が活発化。こうした資金需要の拡大
が金利上昇の一因となり、企業の資金調達コストは増大
 AI関連の投資競争が続くなか、AIブームは企業の選別・再編が進む新たな局面へ。その過程
で経営資源が競争力の高い企業に引き継がれ、AI市場全体の成長は継続すると予想
 性能向上と利用コストの低下により、AIの活用可能な領域は拡大する見込み。ユーザー企業
間の競争がAIの普及を加速させ、市場の持続的な成長を後押し
 AI市場の中核を担う企業の財務は健全。金融システム全体が不安定化するリスクは限定的
 一方、AIインフラ運営企業は将来的な財務負担が高まりやすい構造。特にネオクラウドでは、
減価償却費の負担が利益を圧迫するほか、2030年頃には設備更新や債務借換えが資金繰
りの重荷に。市場による企業評価は一段と厳格化し、投融資先の選別が進む可能性

AI投資ブームはまだ入口。「終わりの始まり」ではなく、「始まりの終わり」
 AI投資ブームは、競争激化に伴い企業の選別・再編が進む新たな局面へ
― マクロでみた設備過剰感は初期段階。今後もモデル開発や計算基盤を巡る競争が激化するなか、資金の出し手や事業
パートナーによる選別は一段と厳格化。一部企業では経営破綻や事業再編が進む可能性
― もっとも、個別企業の淘汰はAI技術の衰退を意味せず。設備・顧客・人材などの経営資源が競争力の高い企業へ再配
分されることで、AIの発展・普及は続く見込み

### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 美國 9 月 ISM 非製造業 PMI 從 8 月的 55.4 降至 54.9，顯示服務業活動增速放
緩，然而國內需求強勁使供應鏈承壓，並推動投入價格指數由 72.6 升至 74.0 為
四年多來新高；另一方面，美國 10 月密西根大學消費者信心指數由 9 月的 48.1
降至 46.3，為連續第三個月下降且創 5 月以來最低 ， 惟未來一年通膨預期由 4.6%
升至 4.7%，顯示通膨可能持續維持高位至 2027 年。
⚫ 聯準會 9 月 FOMC 會議紀要顯示與會官員均支持升息，並認為年底前尚需升息
一次 ，但對於後續升息時點及升息理由上存在分歧； 其中部分官員認為升息是防
止能源及其他價格衝擊造成更廣泛影響 的預防性措施，但更為鷹派官員則認為
升息在於抑制投資和支出，以防範正在顯現的需求驅動型通膨。
⚫ 歐洲央行 9 月貨幣政策會議紀要顯示全體決策委員均同意升息，並認為當前經
濟成長風險偏向下行 ， 通膨風險則偏向上行 ，因此將保留政策彈性， 根據經濟數

據做為決策，不提供未來利率路徑指引。
⚫ 台灣 9 月 CPI 年增率自 8 月 2.05%上升至 2.73%，已連續 5 個月超過 2%通膨警
戒線 ， 且創兩年半來最大漲幅 ，除了豪雨導致蔬果價格上漲及中秋假期的短期因
素外，油價居高不下亦使通膨壓力增加。
⚫ 中國央行發布匯率政策立場聲明， 強調中國貿易發展根源於產業國際競爭力的
提升，無必要通過匯率貶值獲取貿易競爭優勢 ，並指出中國長期實施以市場供需
為基礎、參考一籃子貨幣進行調節管理浮動匯率制度。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Oct/12/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.699，低點在 31.940，以 31.875 作收，較前一週收盤價
貶值 4.5 分。 
上週台幣震盪走貶，週初，受到前週公布的美國非農就業數據低於預期，降低
市場對聯準會 10 月升息的預期， 台股創高吸引外資匯入 ， 推升台幣最高來到31.699；
不過隨著美伊緊張情勢升溫且台灣連假將至 ， 台股收低使外資轉為大幅賣超並匯出 ，
推低台幣回到 9 月中以來的震盪區間下緣，最低觸及 31.940，最終在央行調節之下
收在 31.875。 
＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣區間震盪。就國際市場而言，美國 10 月密西根大學消費者信心
指數持續下滑，由 9 月的 48.1 降至 46.3，創 5 月以來最低，惟未來一年通膨預期
升至 4.7%，加上中東局勢反覆，油價高位震盪，仍支撐市場對於聯準會年內升息
的預期，預計美元指數維持在 102 附近震盪，對台幣帶來壓力；就台灣市場來看，
台灣 9 月出口創歷史新高，年增 60.9%，反映 AI 及科技需求持續強勁，出口商後
續拋匯需求可望提供台幣支撐；然而，台股逼近五萬點關卡之際，外資未能延續買
超走勢，且連假前已大幅賣超台股，若後續外資持續匯出，將限制台幣升值空間。
綜合以上，預估本週台幣區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.700-32.000

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Oct/12/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣區間震盪 。 中國十一長假期間 (10/1~10/7) 境內人民幣市場休市 ， 而
境外人民幣維持偏強格局 ， 主要在6.7000 上方震盪 ， 臨近周末 ， 人行中間價為6.7330，
創 3 年多來新高，加上美元指數漲勢放緩，推升境外人民幣突破 6.7000 整數關卡，
終場境外人民幣收在 6.6927，週線升值 134 點，境內人民幣收在 6.6929，較十一長
假前夕升值 121 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週人民幣區間偏升。就國際市場來看，美國密西根大學消費者信心指數
由 9 月的 48.1 降至 46.3，創5 月以來新低 ， 不過對於未來一年的通膨預期升至4.7%，
加上油價維持高檔 ， 市場對於聯準會年內升息的預期仍存 ， 美元指數維持高檔震盪 ，
將限制人民幣升值速度；就中國國內市場來看，人行中間價維持偏強設定，不過人
行表示將防範短期大幅波動與急貶風險 ， 並無意透過人民幣貶值取得貿易競爭優勢 ，
有助於穩定市場對人民幣的預期 ；最後就技術面來看 ，USD/CNH 日線圖中 ， 各天期
均線仍向下發散，空頭格局不變，下方支撐先看向 9 月底低點 6.6908。綜合以上，
預估本週人民幣區間偏升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6800-6.7100

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Oct/12/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY 震盪走升。週初，因美元指數在102 附近盤整，帶領USD/JPY 延
續前一週走勢震盪走升到 158 上方；週間，因缺乏重要經濟數據，市場偏向觀望，
USD/JPY 維持在 158 附近震盪；臨近週末，USD/JPY 曾一度回測 157.50 附近，不
過在美日利差的主導下，USD/JPY 依舊保持偏多的走勢，終場收在 158.31，週線上
漲 84 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、路透短觀調查顯示，受半導
體強勁訂單推動，日本 10 月大型製造業信心升至正 22，創近五年新高；但原料成
本高漲重擊非製造業，信心指數降至正 23。與此同時，此前投下升息反對票的日銀
鴿派委員佐藤綾野立場軟化，表態支持分階段升息，市場對後續正常化預期升溫，
預計將限縮 USD/JPY 的上行空間 。二 、 日本首相高市早苗表示密切關注匯率與物價
走勢 ， 強調日本已脫離通縮 ， 將完全尊重央行政策獨立性 ， 暗示不反對進一步升息 。
面對通膨加劇與日圓走貶壓力，其過往堅持的貨幣寬鬆立場已出現轉向。然而目前
美日實質利差仍大，持續為 USD/JPY 提供支撐。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，
USD/JPY 持續沿著上彎的月線上攻，然匯價持續承壓於季線下方，在有效站上季線
及突破前高壓力前，仍以區間震盪看待。綜合以上，預估USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震
盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：157.10-159.50。 
前週交易區間

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Oct/12/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 震盪 走跌 。週初， 因法國債券風險與區域政治不確定性 ，
EUR/USD 下行最低來到 1.1160； 週間 ，受中東地緣風險升溫與歐洲財政疑慮帶動避
險需求 ， 加上美債殖利率維持高檔 ， 美元利差優勢延續，歐元維持低檔； 臨近週末 ，
逢低歐元有買盤支撐，帶動歐元收復部分跌幅，終場收在 1.1201，週線下跌 46 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 區間偏空震盪，主要原因如下：一、歐洲央行首席經濟學
家連恩表示，夏末能源成本飆升為經濟增長帶來下行風險，並可能限制央行收緊貨
幣政策的力道，與此同時，歐元區 10 月 Sentix 投資者信心指數由 5.1 降至 2.7，德
國預期指數同步滑落，反映市場對經濟復甦的信心明顯降溫，預計將令歐元持續承
壓。二、法國社會動盪與支出訴求阻礙財政減赤進程，加劇債務可持續性疑慮，並
推升法德公債利差與信用風險，令歐元承壓走弱。相較美國經濟展現韌性且聯準會
維持偏鷹立場，歐洲深陷景氣放緩與財政掣肘，歐洲央行緊縮空間受限。在歐美基
本面與政策前景持續分歧下 ，EUR/USD 缺乏實質支撐 ， 後市將維持偏弱整理並下探
關鍵支撐。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 匯價雖多次向下測試 1.1165 關
鍵價位均獲得支撐，惟每次反彈均無法站上五日線，且各天期均線下彎呈現空頭排
列。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈現區間偏空震盪的可能性較高。

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分 ， 本週邁入新的提存期 ， 央行連日減發存單 ， 令市場資金供
給充裕，帶動短率朝區間低位移動。 
週一央行標售 364 天期定存單 1100 億元 ， 加權平均得標利率為1.602%， 低於
市場預期區間 1.61%-1.635%，較上期標售結果下跌 4.1 個基本點。 
週 四 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.830% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.67%~1.68%，利率大致持平於上週。 
二、 受美債利率續創新高影響，週四 10 年期指標券 A15109 成交在 2.05%，創同
年期券自 2008 年以來高點，5 年期指標券 A15108 則未成交，最近成交利率
為 1.82%。 
台灣 9 月 CPI 年增率自 8 月 2.05%上升至 2.73%，已連續 5 個月超過 2%通膨
警戒線 ， 且創兩年半來最大漲幅 ， 目前油價仍居高難下 ， 通膨壓力將令央行維
持偏緊的貨幣政策，加上國際債市利率持續走升，台債殖利率恐面臨上行壓
力，預估 5 年券區間為 1.85%~1.95%，10 年券區間為 2.0%~2.10%。

市場焦點
美國
• 9月ISM服務業PMI降至54.9，雖商業活動與新訂單放緩 ，但就業回升至 50.1、訂單積壓增加 ，
顯示服務需求仍具韌性；另一方面，價格指數升至74.0，創2022年7月以來新高，供應商交貨
速度亦放緩 ，反映燃料 、原物料及供應鏈成本升高 ，景氣未明顯轉弱之際 ，通膨壓力反而重
新升溫；同時全球長債殖利率同步走高 ，AI基建投資延續企業成長動能 ，也提高資本需求 。
整體而言 ，美國經濟尚未明顯轉弱 ，但通膨與長端利率壓力同步升高 ，使金融條件維持偏緊 。
中國
• 彭博指出在政府積極推動開源策略與 AI普及下，中國產業快速發展出現供給過剩 ，產業獲利
能力面臨挑戰，未來三年中國AI產業將可能處於虧損狀態，不過即使模型價格下降，AI使用量
增加將帶動Token需求大幅成長，其中具有企業客戶、專有資料及生態優勢的AI龍頭，將比單

純提供模型服務的公司更具長期競爭力。
黃金
• 歐美國家財政可持續性的疑慮持續發酵 ，加上長期通膨風險升溫 ，宏觀不確定性讓實體黃金
市場逆勢吸金 。本波下跌主要反映去槓桿交易 ，實體黃金的基本面未變 ，有望為金價帶來中
期的支撐。建議投資人逢修正分批進場 ，可透過黃金存摺或是實體黃金 ETF(GLD)，以投資組
合多元化的角度布局。波段投機者，從技術指標來看，可留意4,000美元關卡是否出現支撐。

美國9月ISM服務需求略降、成本壓力升高，景氣韌性與通膨風險並存
• 服務業維持擴張，需求略降但就業回穩：9月ISM服務業指數降至54.9(前月55.4)，連續27個月位於擴張區間；商業活動
指數降至56.5、新訂單降至 59.8，顯示服務需求較前月放緩 ，但就業指數回升至 50.1、重返擴張區間 ，訂單積壓升至
56.6，顯示內需仍具韌性，服務業尚未出現明顯轉弱訊號。
• 成本與供應鏈壓力升高 ，通膨降溫面臨干擾： 價格指數升至 74.0，創2022年7月以來新高 ，且為7個月來第6次高於70；
供應商交貨指數升至53.2，顯示交貨速度放緩，燃料、柴油、鋼材、銅及記憶體等成本上升，加上供應受限，使服務業
投入成本壓力擴大，通膨降溫進程面臨干擾。
• 市場觀察：美國服務業為經濟成長的重要支撐，目前經濟呈現「需求仍強、通膨升溫」的拉鋸格局，雖服務業景氣支撐

美國企業獲利，但成本上升可能推升利率波動，投資上宜偏向美國短中天期投資級債，兼顧較高收益率與利率風險控管；
股票則可聚焦具定價能力、獲利與現金流穩健的優質企業。

全球利率同步走高、AI投資持續擴張，長端美債壓力仍高
• 全球長債殖利率同步走高，反映利率環境重新定價：美國財政部長貝森特指出，近期美債殖利率攀升與德國、日本等主
要債市同步上行，反映全球資本成本重新定價，而非市場對美國資產失去信心；加上目前尚未出現資金大規模撤離美債
並轉向其他主權債券的現象，因此債市波動較偏向全球利率環境調整，而非金融系統性風險。
• AI投資增加資金需求，支撐較高利率環境：貝森特亦駁斥AI泡沫論，認為微軟、Alphabet及Meta等大型科技企業仍以
實際獲利與現金流支撐持續擴大AI資本支出，不同於過去缺乏基本面的科技泡沫；龐大的資料中心 、電力與半導體投資
需求提高整體資金成本，也成為推升長天期利率的重要因素之一。
• 市場觀察：目前市場經濟近況屬於利率高檔、成長尚穩的情境中，因而投資策略建議配置大型科技股、AI基建供應鏈及

受惠利差環境的金融股具相對優勢；債券配置則以中短天期投資級債優先，避免長天期債券受利率上行壓力侵蝕。

• 美國8月貿易逆差擴大至2025年初以來最高水平：美國提高進口關稅後，貿易失衡問題仍未明顯改善，商務部數據顯示，8
月貿易逆差擴大13.7%至1,056億美元，且超出市場預期的1,021億美元。經通膨調整後，8月商品貿易逆差擴大至1,147億美
元，同樣為2025年3月以來最大。
• 觀察美國本土投資能否進一步轉化為出口能力：AI建設帶動半導體與設備進口，加上能源成本上升及關稅政策干擾，推升進
口額至歷史新高。不過，強勁內需也顯示消費與企業投資仍具支撐力。隨著第三季財報季即將展開，根據Factset數據，分析
師預期第三季標普500企業獲利年增約29.5%，加上企業獲利預期持續上修，強化投資人對高利率環境下企業基本面的信心。
投資策略上，相較於單筆投資，此時的高成長與高波動的環境，更適合分批布局的定期定額策略來參與市場。

向循環，同時也強化日本央行持續推進貨幣政策正常化的條件。雖然升息可能推升日圓與資金成本，但金融股可受惠於利差
擴大，且企業治理改革、AI投資及自動化需求仍提供獲利支撐。近期日股AI與半導體族群已明顯走強，顯示市場仍重視企業
獲利成長；中長期建議維持日股正向看法，聚焦金融、AI、機器人、自動化及電力設備等具結構性成長題材的企業。

中國AI產業陷入逐底競爭困境
• 中國AI產業陷入逐底競爭困境 ：彭博指出在政府積極推動開源策略與
AI普及下，中國AI產業快速發展 ，但也逐漸出現供給過剩 ，使產業獲
利能力面臨挑戰 。開源模式雖有助於降低技術門檻 、應用普及 ，但也
使技術優勢難以長期維持 ，加上中國市場長期習慣低價甚至免費的數
位服務，多數AI業者難以透過訂閱模式獲利，轉而依賴利潤較低的API
服務作為主要收入來源 。目前中國已備案大型語言模型 (LLM)數量達
988個，包括DeepSeek、Kimi及智譜AI等新創企業快速崛起，進一步
加劇市場競爭。此外API服務逐漸被視為基礎公用服務，即使AI Agent
帶動詞元(Token)需求快速成長，也未必能同步提升獲利，由於開源模
型可由不同雲端業者部署 ，市場進入門檻降低 ，業者更難建立長期競

爭優勢。
• 後市展望： 考量龐大資本支出與持續價格競爭 ，未來三年中國 AI產業
整體仍可能處於虧損狀態 ，市場將形成「美國閉源、高毛利」與「中
國開源 、低成本 」模式 ，即使模型價格下降 ，AI 使用量增加將帶動
Token需求大幅成長 ，進而推升資料中心 、雲端服務及 AI晶片需求 ，
從投資角度來看，基礎設施供應商(晶片、伺服器、資料中心、雲端)受
惠程度可能高於模型開發商；而具有企業客戶 、專有資料及生態優勢
的AI龍頭，將比單純提供模型服務的公司更具長期競爭力。

中國已備案的LLM數量

人行下調抵押補充貸款利率和房貸貼息等政策出台，有望提振中國經濟
和市場信心
• 人行下調抵押補充貸款利率和房貸貼息等政策出台 ，有望提振中國經濟和市場信心 ：日前中國推出購房貸款貼息政策及下調
PSL(抵押補充貸款)利率，代表穩增長政策進一步加碼 ，有助於提振市場信心與經濟預期 ，此次政策不僅支持房地產市場 ，也
將「六張網」建設、科技創新、民營及小微企業 ，納入重點扶持範圍 ，透過增加再貸款額度 ，與提高支持比例 ，帶動投資、
就業及消費回升。短期A股有望在政策推動下出現築底反彈，其中基建投資、製造業投資、房市銷售及居民消費有機會改善，
同時PSL(抵押補充貸款)降息有助於流動性環境邊際寬鬆 ，降低市場對經濟增長的疑慮 ，後市來看企業獲利與信用環境有望逐
步修復，進一步鞏固A股慢牛格局。投資建議上房貸貼息政策有利房地產、建材、家居等產業鏈，金融業受惠資金成本下降與

信貸回升，食品飲料、零售、家電及服務消費則可望受惠於居民消費意願提升。

中國、日本減持美國公債的背後邏輯與差異
• 橋水基金創始人達利歐警告 ，美國公債正面臨中日兩國減持的風險 ，數據顯示這項警告已成事實 。2026年前七個月，中國
美國公債持倉減少660億美元至6,180億美元；同期日本持倉亦減少820億美元至1.1兆美元，扣除價格變動的估值效應後，
兩國實質持倉均大幅下滑。然而，兩國削減美國公債的背後動機並不相同，這將導致未來的減持軌跡出現明顯分化。
• 中國戰略性長期去美元化：1.降低地緣政治風險與轉向資產多元化 ，隨著人民幣在國際貿易結算中使用增加 ，市場流動性提
升，中國對美元儲備依賴度顯著下降。2.美債財政可持續性隱憂，美國政府債務持續攀升，引發對美國長期財政健康疑慮。
3.根據彭博經濟研究模型預測，在漸進式減持情境下，中國的美元儲備(以美債與機構債為主)到2050年可能減少約50%。

• 中國搭配戰術性節奏調整 (短期緩和因素)：儘管長期趨勢向下 ，但目前中美利差顯著 (美中30年期公債利差超過 350個基點)，
在美債殖利率維持高位期間，過快減持意味著實現債券價格下跌的虧損，且可能加劇人民幣升值壓力。因此，中國短中期內
傾向採取「戰略性減持、戰術性放緩」的靈活步調。
美國公債持倉：中國 vs 日本
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.8，美國財政部
中國預計將減少美元儲備
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.8，中國央行、國際貨幣基金組織、彭博經濟研究。

日本受政策利率與本土資本回流驅動
• 與中國的結構性考量不同，日本的減持主要出自週期性因素與匯率干預需求。
• 日本央行貨幣政策轉鷹： 日本央行正加快升息腳步 。隨著日債殖利率上揚 (30年期日債殖利率升至約 4.2%)，美日利差持續
收窄，吸引日本國內機構資金回流本土主權債市場。
• 匯率干預政策：日本為防範日圓過度貶值，日本財務省傾向出售部分海外美債資產以籌集外匯干預資金。若日圓再次面臨壓
力，此類拋售干預可能持續。因此，隨著日本央行逐步走向政策正常化，日本對美債的持倉大概會延續下滑軌跡。
• 小結與投資建議：美國公債作為全球無風險資產的地位正面臨結構性挑戰，中國的「長期去美元化」與日本的「本土利率回
升資金回流」將形成雙重壓力，這意味著美債殖利率短期內難大幅下行，美國財政部發債成本居高不下，進而推高全球資本

的融資成本。投資債券配置建議採取「中短天期＋階梯式配置」策略，長天期美國公債(20年以上)受發債供給過剩及中日兩
國長期減持影響，價格波動較大。建議投資人適度降低長天期美國公債暴險，轉向防禦性較佳的中短天期美國公債或高品質
高評級公司債，以鎖定當前較高殖利率同時控管利率風險。
殖利率差：美元與人民幣 vs 美元與日圓
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.29

黃金市場資金流向分歧，實體投資有望支撐中長期金價表現
• 利率與匯率因素，壓抑金價近期表現：9月以來，美國公債殖利率飆升、美元指數走揚，由於黃金本身不孳息，資金傾向轉
往固定收益市場，疊加美元走強壓抑國際購買意願，金價自8月底高點至今已下修約11%。
• 9月資金流向分歧，實體投資強、期貨市場弱：根據世界黃金協會統計，9月在金價下跌之際，全球實體黃金ETF卻吸引大量
資金流入，流入約100億美元(67噸)。另一方面，COMEX黃金期貨市場投機部位大幅下降 ，避險基金與其他機構投資人的
多頭部位明顯減少，造成期貨市場賣壓增加，進一步推動金價下跌。
• 實體黃金市場逆勢吸金反應投資人的避險需求：歐美國家財政可持續性的疑慮持續發酵，加上美伊衝突未解及AI算力需求強
勁，長期通膨風險升溫，宏觀的不確定性讓實體黃金市場逆勢吸金。從數據上來看，本波下跌主要反映去槓桿交易，實體黃

金的基本面未變 ，有望為金價帶來中期的支撐 。建議投資人逢修正分批進場 ，可透過黃金存摺或是實體黃金 ETF(GLD)，以
投資組合多元化的角度布局。波段投機者，從技術指標來看，可留意4,000美元關卡是否出現支撐。
資料來源：世界黃金協會，2026.10.7。

非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。
11. 屬基金資訊觀測站ESG基金專區所列之基金，有關基金之ESG資訊，投資人應於申購前詳閱基金公開說明書或投資人須知所載之基金所有特色或目標等資訊。
警 語

## Document 147: 261105_ml_kyec

### Page 1

King Yuan Electronics Corp.

Testing business diversification strengthen 
’27 outlook at low-end valuation

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 330.00 TWD | Price: 228.50 TWD

3Q26 results in line; 4Q TPU ramp offsets GPU headwind 
3Q26 sales were +9% QoQ backed by healthy demand from its GPU/CPU customers and 
early mobile inventory builds. With higher depreciation and labor cost moving to COGS 
as new facility production kick off, GM dipped 190bps QoQ to 37.6% in 3Q26 but 
operating leverage lifted OpM 40bps QoQ to 25.8%, taking EPS to NT$2.06. 4Q26 sales 
is guided +7-9% QoQ, in line with our preview, due to slower GPU ramp from continued 
HBM4 yield issue at Hynix partially offsetting strength in AI ASICs (Google’s v8t/i TPUs) 
and CPUs (Nvidia Vera, Google Axion and Microsoft Cobalt). Despite better business mix, 
the rising cost and slower UTR improvement should keep GMs stable at 36-37% in 4Q. 
Solid position into 2027 across chipset and wafer testing 
KYEC’s ’26 sales is now on track to grow 34% YoY and could further accelerate in ’27 at 
+53% YoY from a diversifying application base across GPU, CPU, networking switches and 
ASICs. It is also in a strong position to capture its foundry partner’s chip probing 
outsourcing demand in addition to higher testing unit/dollar content from its existing 
direct customer business. To support expansion across wafer sorting, chip testing and 
system level testing, the company is accelerating its clean room construction in Taiwan 
and has announced it will grow its testing footprint in the US. While its ’27 capex could 
stay at elevated levels, we think the investment mix shifting toward facility should keep its 
depreciation under control. This along with capex-light tool consignment business model 
could drive its GMs to 40%+ in 1H27 and keep profitability resilient through the cycles. 
Recent pullback to low-end valuation: particularly attractive 
We keep EPS for ‘26/27/28E largely unchanged at NT$7.87/NT$15.36/NT$19.18, and 
reiterate our Buy rating with PO of NT$330 still based on 18x 2H27-1H28E P/E (vs. its 
10-25x P/E range). Following the AI sentiment drag, the stock is trading at 15x/11x 
‘27/28E P/E, attractive as the business downside is protected by its leadership in scale 
and execution for AI chipset testing even if AI investment moderates in ‘28.

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
Founded in 1987 and headquartered in Hsinchu, Taiwan, 
King Yuan Electronics Corp. (KYEC) is a niche Outsourced 
Semiconductor Assembly and Test (OSAT) company 
focused on IC testing services, with market shares of 3% in 
OSAT (IC packaging+testing, number 7 vendor) and 14% in 
IC testing (number 2 vendor) globally. Its capacity is mainly 
located in Greater China. In addition to IC testing, it has 
c.20% revenue exposure to IC packaging, mainly at its 
Chinese subsidiary and mainly for memory products.

Investment Rationale 
We have a Buy rating on KYEC, in view of undervalued 
upside from smartphone business for both near-term and 
longer-term, structural opportunities in AI areas led by GPU, 
as well as the solid GPM/ROE expansion toward into 2025.

### Page 5

Price objective basis & risk 
King Yuan Electronics Corp. (KYUFF) 
We value KYEC at NT$330 per share, based on 18x 2H27-1H28E P/E, which is close to 
its historical average since 2010. In our view, P/E valuation is appropriate for KYEC given 
its strong growth in earnings over the past few years, future growth potentials with 
upside in total addressable market (TAM) and market share, and enhanced cash position. 
We think 18x P/E, which is above the average P/E level of global OSAT peers, is 
undemanding, in view of its better ROE profile and 2-3% dividend yield.

Downside risks are 
1) faster-than-expected pick-up by testing peers in high-end AI area, 
2) greater-than-expected slowdown in global smartphone/consumer electronics demand, 
3) potential asset disposal caused by strategy shift amid the ongoing decoupling in the 
semis supply chain.

## Document 163: 261107_ml_wiwynn

### Page 1

Wiwynn

AI-driven sales growth since 4Q; reiterate 
Buy and trim PO to NT$2,310

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 2,310 TWD | Price: 1,555 TWD

3Q beat driven by both general and ASIC servers 
Wiwynn’s 3Q sales grew 56% QoQ/63% YoY, supported by sustained general server 
demand as well as a strong ramp of ASIC servers (e.g., AWS Trainium 3) from Sep. 
GM/OPM of 8.87%/7.47% was above BofAe of 7.77%/6.42% and consensus of 
8.42%/6.85%. OP was 40%/16% above BofAe/consensus. After a non-op. loss (mainly 
from higher interest expenses), NP was 34%/10% above BofAe/consensus.  
4Q sales led by ASIC; GPU sales expected by year-end 
The company posted strong Oct sales of NT$165.1bn (-4% MoM/+81% YoY), tracking 
35%/36% of BofA/consensus estimates. Its general server business was impacted by 
component shortages in Oct, despite demand remaining strong. For AI servers, we 
expect ASIC to be the major sales driver in 4Q with a strong ramp since Sep; while GPU 
servers (from multiple customers) are expected to deliver sales contribution by year-end.  
Solid sales growth in 2027-28; GM pressured by AI  
Our checks indicate that Wiwynn’s ASIC projects remain on a buy-and-sell model, 
although a consignment business model is under discussion; while business models for 
GPU projects have yet to be confirmed. With strong AI server momentum, we expect its 
GM to decline QoQ to 7.67% in 4Q26. For 2027/28, we model solid YoY sales growth of 
+60%/+31% , with conservative GMs of 6.67%/6.51% in 2027/28, vs. 8.30% in 2026E.  
Reiterate Buy; trim PO to NT$2,310 
We raise our 2026-28E EPS by 4-14%, mainly to reflect the 3Q26 beat and solid sales 
growth aided by general and ASIC server projects. We however trim PO to NT$2,310 
(15x 4Q26-3Q27E EPS) from NT$2,387 (18x 2H26-1H27E EPS), as we roll over our 
valuation base and lower multiple on macro uncertainties and concerns over margin 
decline. Reit. Buy on robust ASIC momentum and a constructive general server outlook.

Company Sector 
IT Hardware

Company Description 
Wiwynn supplies server, storage and cloud equipment to 
global cloud service providers (CSPs). 100% of its sales 
comes from server/datacenter business. Key clients are 
Meta, Microsoft, and Amazon. Wiwynn was founded in April 
2012 and was listed on Taiwan Stock Exchange on 27 
March 2019. As of 2Q26, Wistron (3231 TT) owned 35.27% 
of shares in Wiwynn. Besides the headquarter in Taiwan, its 
subsidiaries and operations are located in Malaysia, Hong 
Kong, China, South Korea, Japan, the US and Mexico.

Investment Rationale 
We have a Buy rating on Wiwynn as we are positive on 
increasing contribution from AI server projects, delivery in 
Amazon after efforts for years, and regular server 
bottoming out from 2H23, driving double-digit 
sales/earnings growth in 2026-28E.

### Page 5

Price objective basis & risk 
Wiwynn (XWIQF) 
Our PO of NT$2,310 is based on 15x 4Q26-3Q27E EPS, which is 1.5-2.0SD above 
Wiwynn's historical P/E average since listing. We believe such a multiple is reasonable to 
reflect Wiwynn's rising sales contribution from AI server, delivery in Amazon projects, 
and regular server demand bottoming out from 2H23.

Upside risks to our PO are: (1) higher-than-expected capex growth from key clients, (2) 
stronger-than-expected momentum in server/datacenter infrastructure, (3) more project 
wins/launches for AI server, and (4) much higher contribution from the third largest 
customer.

Downside risks to our PO are: (1) unexpected slowdown of key clients' capex spending in 
server/datacenter, (2) more intensified competition from peers, and (3) weaker 
momentum in AI server projects.

FUNDAMENTAL EQUITY OPINION KEY: Opinions include a Volatility Risk Rating, an Investment Rating and an Income Rating. VOLATILITY RISK RATINGS, indicators 
of potential price fluctuation, are: A - Low, B - Medium and C - High. INVESTMENT RATINGS reflect the analyst’s assessment of both a stock’s absolute total return 
potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment ratings are: 1 -  Buy stocks 
are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or 
increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment 
rating of 6 (No Rating) indicates that a stock is no longer trading on the basis of fundamentals. Analysts assign investment ratings considering, among other things, 
the 0-12 month total return expectation for a stock and the firm’s guidelines for ratings dispersions (shown in the table below). The current price objective for a 
stock should be referenced to better understand the total return expectation at any given time. The price objective reflects the analyst’s view of the potential price 
appreciation (depreciation).

### Page 1

Simplo Technology (6121.TWO)
An Established Battery Franchise with Room for a Better Mix
CITI'S TAKE 
3Q26 EPS missed our/BBG estimates by 35%/30% each as notebook pull-
ins faded and low yields on a major US customer’s new smartphone held 
back shipments. We expect higher PC component costs to keep IT demand 
subdued in 1Q27E, while rising contribution from AES could still help margin 
expansion in 2027E. We lower 2026-2028E EPS by 17-20% and revise our 
TP to NT$400 based on 12x 2027E EPS, lowered from 13x reflecting weaker 
visibility on IT recovery. We continue to favor Simplo’s leading market share 
in notebook/smartphone battery franchises and its exposure to AIDC BBU 
growth through AES. 
3Q earnings -17% y/y missed our/BBG estimates by 35%/30% on weaker IT 
shipments — 3Q26 sales of NT$19.9bn fell 2% y/y and 5% q/q, 13%/10% below 
our/BBG forecasts. Notebook customers pulled orders into 1H26 amid rising 
memory prices, while lower yields on a major US customer’s new smartphone 
constrained 3Q shipment. Group GPM rose 0.7ppt y/y to 16.3% on a larger AES 
contribution. However, standalone GPM dropped 1.6ppt y/y 50 9.2% on higher input 
costs and lower volumes. Group OPM rose 0.4ppt y/y to 9.7%, lower than our (12.2%) 
and BBG (11.9%) estimates. Higher taxes also weighed on EPS. 
2027E outlook stays muted for IT — The mgmt. expects IT demand to remain 
lukewarm in 1Q27E as higher component costs put upward pressure on PC prices 
likely weighing on demand. Standalone sales are expected to be flat to slightly down 
y/y in 2027E, with the group sales flat to slightly up on AES’s BBU growth. Mgmt. 
notes the battery-pack inventories at notebook brands are lean at just a few weeks 
and easing cobalt prices could bring down ASP in 2027E. A new non-IT product line 
is growing severalfold from a small base, and the mgmt. expects it to make up >5% 
in 2028E with a GPM above group’s average. 
Implications — We cut 2026-2028E earnings estimates 17-20% for 2026-2028E 
reflecting 3Q miss and a slower IT recovery, resulting in a 10-18% cut on our sales 
forecasts. We lower our TP to NT$400 based on a target P/E of 12x from 13x on 2027E 
EPS, reflecting the component inflation likely delaying the timing of IT recovery. We 
stay Buy on Simplo’s established notebook and smartphone battery franchises and 
AES’s exposure to growing datacenter BBU demand. AI PC adoption could lift 
battery-pack ASPs over time, and an estimated 6% dividend yield should help 
cushion downside.

Simplo Technology (6121.TWO)
09 November 2026 Citi Research

Simplo Technology
Company description
Established in 1992, Simplo specializes in designing and producing battery
packs for Notebooks, tablet PC, and electronic vehicle products. With strong
execution and cost advantages, Simplo continues to broaden its client base.
Its major customers include PC OEMs (such as HPQ, Dell, Asus, Acer, and
Apple) and NB ODMs (such as Quanta, Compal, Wistron, and Inventec).

Investment strategy
We rate Simplo shares as Buy. Simplo’s heating fundamentals are driven by
the following reasons. 1) Japan supplier's strategy change on focusing EV
battery pack and de-prioritizing NB which provides Simplo more orders wins;
and 2)  Non-IT: according to our industry survey, Simplo has been qualified by
global ISPs for the battery back units (BBU) for their data centers.
Valuation
Our target price of NT$400 is based on a target P/E of 12x 2027E EPS, on par

with its historical average since 2010.
Risks
Downside risks that may cause the shares to continue to trade below our
target price include: 1) weaker than expected end product sales; 2) intensified
competition; and 3) worse-than-expected overall NB/tablet/PC growth.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

## Document 180: 261109_ms_chenbro-DG

Source PDF: all report pdf/261109_ms_chenbro-DG.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-09
Pages: 16
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

M
Idea
Muted momentum in near term: Chenbro's 3Q26 earnings fell short of market
expectations by ~30% on lower revenue and margins, owing to customer inventory
digestion, a longer qualification process for its ASIC customer's new product and a
less favorable revenue mix. Looking to 4Q26,  management guided for revenue to
stay roughly flat QoQ (Oct at NT$2,308mn, -7% MoM/+16% YoY) and GM to be
similar to that in 3Q26, as the overhang of new product qualification extends into

4Q26.
2027 still seen as a good year: We believe it continue to grow along with the new
AI server platforms, its customer's share gains and incremental contribution from
new products. We now model for revenue to grow by 35% YoY, GM to be roughly
flat at 29.3% and EPS to rise 39% YoY for 2027.
More diversification into the AI clusters: Chenbro has been leveraging its existing
expertise in mechanical components and customer relationship to diversify from

chassis into other mechanical components in AI applications. It started to see more
meaningful contribution from racks at 5-10% in 2025, with ~10% likely in 2026 and
potentially topping 20% in 2027. For the longer term, it could step into other
mechanical components in the liquid cooling space. Although these efforts could
open up more opportunities for Chenbro to grow, they don't all carry higher-than-
average margins, so potential dilution could be a factor to monitor.

Global footprint continues to expand: To improve its supply chain resilience and
local production capability, Chenbro has several capacity expansion plans globally,
with  Taiwan remaining  the hub for high-end production and R&D. In the pipeline,
Malaysia will likely see new capacity ramping from 4Q26, Vietnam from 2H27 and
US pilot run/MP in 4Q27/2H28. Capex was guided at NT$2.5-3.0bn for 2026.
Downgrade to EW: We believe Chenbro should remain one of the competitive

players in the AI mechanical component space, but near-term hiccups might mute
business momentum. Despite valuation approaching its trough, we see no strong
catalyst in the coming quarters. We thus reduce our rating to EW and lower our PT
by 61% to NT$630 (12x 2027e P/E, implying a PEG of 0.3 vs. the average of 0.5 in
2020-25).
What’s Changed

in the near term, and thus downgrade the stock.

Results Summary
Off revenue of NT$7,395mn (-5% QoQ/+31% YoY), GM contracted by 6.8ppt QoQ to
25.8%, mainly due to a less-favorable product mix and costs associated with new product
qualification. This was short of MSe at 31.9% and consensus at 30.2%, or by 6.1ppt and
4.3ppt, respectively.
Opex was NT$546mn (opex ratio of 7.4%) vs. MSe of NT$686mn (opex ratio 8.2%) and
consensus of NT$617mn (opex ratio 7.5%), leading to operating profit of NT$1,373mn

(OPM 18.6%). This was 31% lower than MSe at NT$1,996mn (OPM 23.9%) and 26% lower
than consensus at NT$1,854mn (OPM 22.6%).
With a non-op gain of NT$2mn (FX gain of NT$18mn), net profit of NT$1,005mn (EPS NT
$8.21) was 33% lower than MSe at NT$1,500mn (EPS NT$12.25) and 29% below
consensus at NT$1,412mn (EPS NT$11.25).

6
Valuation Methodology
Our price target of NT$630 (previously NT$1,600) is derived from 12x 2027e P/E (vs.
27x 2027e previously): We use price/earnings as our valuation methodology because we
believe it appropriately captures Chenbro's earnings growth potential. We lower our
target multiple to factor in weaker market sentiment toward the stock on softer
fundamental performance and overall to AI names, as well as muted near-term business
momentum. We believe our target P/E multiple of 12x is justified by an earnings CAGR of

32% seen for 2025-28e, implying a PEG of 0.4. This is lower than the PEG of 0.8 in 2014-
24, when the stock traded at an average P/E of 12x for a 15% earnings CAGR.
Bull case scenario value - NT$1,600 (-29%): We apply a 30x P/E multiple (38x 2027e
previously) to our base case estimate for 2027. This scenario mainly assumes a better
outlook for AI capex and more project wins from  new customers.
Bear case scenario value - NT$380 (-42%): We apply a 7x P/E multiple (11x 2027e

previously) to our base case estimate for 2027. This scenario mainly assumes lukewarm
datacenter demand amid softening IT capex budgets and slower progress in its new
product offerings.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

## Document 297: [402340] SK스퀘어(402340) SK하이닉스 최대주주SK스퀘어(402340) SK하이닉스 최대주주SK하이닉스 최대주주 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[402340] SK스퀘어(402340) SK하이닉스 최대주주SK스퀘어(402340) SK하이닉스 최대주주SK하이닉스 최대주주 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

SK 스퀘어 (402340/KS) 
SK 하이닉스 최대주주 
SK 하이닉스 4 분기도 배당 주당 20,000 원 이상  
SK 하이닉스는 전일 주당 20,000원(실적반영: 10,000원, 키옥시아 특별배당: 
10,000원)의 3분기 배당을 발표하였다. 키옥시아 특별배당은 3분기 배당과 26년 회
계연도 결산배당으로 나누어 지급예정이다. 이에 SK스퀘어는 2조 9,220억원(1분기
와 2분기 배당수입은 각 548억원)의 배당수입을 수취하게 된다. SK하이닉스는 26년 
전체 현금 배당 규모는 30조원 이상을 발표했는데, 3분기까지 지급하는 배당금은 14.6
조원이기 때문에 4분기에도 최소한 주당 20.000원 이상의 배당이 예상된다. 이 경우 
SK 스퀘어의 26 년 배당수입은 6 조원에 달할 것으로 예상한다. SK 스퀘어는 ‘26년
~’28년 경상배당수입의 30% 이상을 주주에게 환원하는 주주환원정책을 보유하고 있
어 SK스퀘어의 현 시가총액 대비 27년 주주환원율은 최소 1.5% 이상 될 전망이다.

SK 하이닉스 자사주 취득·소각으로 높아질 지배력 
SK하이닉스는 20조원의 자사주 매입을 발표하였다. 이 중 10조원은 임직원 보상목적
이며, 10조원은 주주환원 목적이다. 26년 총 60조원 이상의 자사주 취득·소각 예정(8
월 결의분 40 조원 포함)으로 임직원 보상 목적의 자사주 매입은 제외되기 때문에 26
년 실적 확정 후 추가적으로 10조원 이상의 자사주 취득이 예상된다. 기 취득한 자사주 
40조원(3.3%)는 11월 5일 소각 예정으로 소각 이후 SK스퀘어의 SK하이닉스 지분
율은 20.5%(2 분기말 기준)에서 21.2%로 상승하게 된다. 이번 발표한 10 조원
(1.0%) 자사주 소각시 SK 스퀘어의 지분율은 21.4%이 되며, 추가로 10 조원을 추가 
소각한다면 21.7%까지 지분율이 상승하게 된다.

SK 하이닉스 대비 높은 베타 
SK스퀘어에 대한 투자의견 매수를 유지한다. SK스퀘어 NAV의 98%가량을 차지하
는 SK하이닉스의 현금배당 증가로 SK스퀘어의 주주환원 재원이 확대되고, SK하이닉
스의 자사주 취득·소각으로 지배력이 강화되기 때문이다. SK하이닉스는 전일 컨퍼런스 
콜을 통해 29년까지 D램과 HBM 물량이 사실상 모두 솔드아웃된 상황이라 밝히며 주
가 반등 가능성이 높은데, SK 스퀘어 베타(1.8)는 SK 하이닉스(1.7) 대비 높기 때문에 
SK 하이닉스 주가 반등에 따른 SK 스퀘어의 동반 주가 상승을 기대할 수 있다. 현재 
SK스퀘어의 NAV 대비 할인율은 46.5%이며, SK하이닉스가 자사주60조원 소각시 
할인율은 49.4%까지 상승하게 된다.

2026-10-29

지주회사/통신서비스/ESG. 최관순 / ks1.choi@sks.co.kr / 3773-8812

COMPLIANCE NOTICE 
작성자(최관순)는 본 조사분석자료에 게재된 내용들이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 신의성실하게 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 보고서에 언급된 종목의 경우 당사 조사분석담당자는 본인의 담당종목을 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
본 보고서는 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공된 사실이 없습니다. 
당사는 자료공표일 현재 해당기업과 관련하여 특별한 이해 관계가 없습니다. 
종목별 투자의견은 다음과 같습니다. 
투자판단 3 단계(6 개월기준) 15%이상 → 매수 / -15%~15% → 중립 / -15%미만 → 매도

## Document 346: Chartpack _ Advanced economies_ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-10-06)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Chartpack _ Advanced economies_ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-10-06).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

By Advanced Economies Team
Advanced Economies:
Resilience to shock after shock
October 2026

### Page 2

• The global economy is in the midst of a growth cycle. As a result, GDP growth is more resilient to shocks than initially
anticipated.
• The figures for the second quarter bear this out, despite the sharp rise in oil prices, whose negative impact on growth is offset
by a series of reflationary forces.
• The driving force behind the current growth cycle is clear: artificial intelligence (AI).
• The world’s major economies are currently engaged in a broader push to expand their capital goods investment.

• While European and US households are experiencing more or less the same energy price shock, inflation and household
consumption remain higher in the US.
• Higher and above-target inflation calls for monetary tightening.
• Inflation is exceeding the target set by three of the four central banks examined in this publication (the US, the UK, the
Eurozone). Inflation is currently expected to peak in Q4 2026 or Q1 2027, but there is a significant risk that energy inflation will

spread further to other components of inflation in the future.
• Inflation can also be a sign of (too much) economic strength (in the US).
• Monetary policies also have to offset the inflationary effects of fiscal policy (particularly in Japan).
• The outbreak of the conflict in Iran acted as a catalyst: the ensuing rise in energy prices contributes to the recent increase in
long-term rates (which had been trending downwards at the start of the year) to levels close to those of 2002.

• The other issue is the high level of fiscal deficits (too high for a period of growth), reflecting different fiscal trajectories. In the
US, the UK, France and Germany the public debt-to-GDP ratio is increasing, while it has stabilized in Italy and decreased in
Spain and Japan.
• The rise in long-term interest rates will weigh on public finances. The fiscal arithmetic is simpler today than it will be in 2030.
By 2030, the effective rate will have risen and caught up with GDP growth.

For more information, please refer to our publication:
EcoPerspectives – Advanced Economies | 3rd quarter of 2026, as of September 24, 2026– Economic Research– BNP Paribas
Advanced economies: Resilience to shock after shock

STRONG RESILIENCE TO EXTERNAL SHOCKS
, 
SOLID 
MACROECONOMIC FUNDAMENTALS 
◼ 
 Over the past 
 two years
 , India has demonstrated remarkable resilience in the face of external shocks, while its 
macroeconomic fundamentals have remained solid.  
◼ 
 In FY2025/2026  
(ended in March 2026)
 , India was hit by a sharp rise in US tariffs. However, thanks to supportive fiscal 
and monetary policies, 
 real GDP  
grew by 7.8% in FY2026, placing India among the fastest
 -
 growing Asian economies.  
◼ 
 For the current fiscal year, the Indian economy is facing rising energy prices 
 (
due to 
 the conflict in the Middle East and 
increased food prices 
 because 
 of 
an 
unfavourable monsoon
 ) 
as well as 
 monetary tightening. Nevertheless, despite these 
new challenges, real GDP growth is expected to exceed 7%.  
◼ 
 India is currently in a stronger position than it was in 2022 to cope with the new energy shock. The fiscal leeway to support  
the economy has increased with the decline in the fiscal deficit, and inflation is more contained.  
◼ 
 Although 
 rising  
energy prices have put pressure on external accounts, FX reserves 
 remain  
sufficient to cover the country’s 
short
 -
 term external financing needs. 
 Yet, 
downward pressures on the rupee have been 
 significant 
 and 
could persist in the 
short term
 . 
In 
H1 
2026, India’s external accounts withstood the rise in energy prices fairly well
 , notably  
thanks to the sharp increase 
in workers’ remittances. 
 T
he 
widening of the trade deficit was primarily due to the substantial increase in machinery and 
equipment imports 
 while t
 he oil trade deficit increased 
 only 
moderately, as the rise in energy prices was offset by a decline 
in import volumes. 
 However, 
in H2 2026, the oil trade deficit is expected to widen significantly.  
Additionally, large portfolio outflows have exacerbated downward pressure on the rupee, necessitating interventions by 
the central bank in foreign exchange markets and the adoption of regulatory measures to support the currency. Although 
these measures hav
 e led to a rebound in the rupee (at least temporarily), inflationary pressures, volatile portfolio 
investments, and low net foreign direct investments are 
 expected to continue 
 to put pressure on the currency.  
◼ 
 D
ownward pressures on the rupee 
 and 
 rising bond yields will reduce the government's 
 already limited 
 fiscal capacity to 
increase public investment and 
 support economic development. However, this will not undermine its ability to refinance 
its debt, given its safe structure.  
◼ 
 T
he banking sector is sufficiently robust to cope with the expected rise in credit risks.

STRONG ECONOMIC GROWTH DESPITE 
STRUCTURAL CONSTRAINTS 
◼ 
 In October 2026, the IMF revised upward 
 India’s  
medium
 -
 term growth 
 forecast 
 from 6.5% to 6.8%  
per year
 . Nonetheless, 
to achieve such strong growth performance, 
 India will have to adapt to the challenges posed by artificial intelligence  
(AI)
, 
while its infrastructure and education system are not prepared for it.  
◼ 
 Moreover, India’s development remains highly uneven. 
 The middle class is still modest
 . This is explained by a large 
informal economy, heavy concentration of employment in low value
 -
 added sectors such as agriculture, low participation 
rates among women and youth, and limited industrial development. All this, in turn, reflects India’s weak i
 ntegration into 
global value chains.  
◼ 
 India faces two key structural challenges: i
 ) adapting the services sector to AI and ii) accelerating industrial development. 
Even with progress in sectors such as smartphones and pharmaceuticals, India’s industrial base remains too small to 
absorb its vast labour force. 
 Labo
u
r market 
 rigidities  
and the mismatch between training and business needs are holding 
back both the creation of quality jobs and industrial development.  
◼ 
 Low net foreign direct investment is a 
 major  
 obstacle. While 
 India attracts 
 new 
 FDI, retention is weak
 : 
 despite 
improvements in logistics, governance issues and regulatory barriers have 
 led 
 disinvestments to triple over the past five 
years.  
◼ 
 India 
 may fail in completing its 
 transition to a higher
 -
 value, more industrialised and inclusive economy if reforms do not 
accelerate 
 significantly
 . Structural change
 s 
could lag behind pressures from its rapidly growing population and 
 fast 
global 
technological advances
 . 
02 November 2026
